i-8CMBvEkgLgkWXKmcyeFqqC	Madam President, I would like I commend the moderate reservations 7 and 8. This is a long-awaited bill, but thank you for what it's been waiting. Unfortunately, this is a hallmark of the current government. The bill is on the whole good, but it has its flaws. Firstly, the age assessment should be carried out immediately upon arrival in Sweden. It is important that children are treated properly and receive the support they need. It must be a shocking journey to travel over the earth alone, without mother or father. This means that it is important, and sometimes absolutely necessary, to provide these children with support and treatment. We need to use every penny of the resources available to these children. Therefore, those funds must go to children and not to adults who say they are children. There may also be unnecessary risks of mixing children, without parents present, with other adults who are not relatives. That is why we Moderates consider it more than reasonable, for the sake of children, that the age assessment should be carried out immediately upon arrival. Secondly, we know that it has been difficult for the Migration Agency to get age determinations in place. This has largely been due to the fact that the county councils have been responsible for such measures, and there the interest – to say the least – has been cool. We Moderates therefore believe that it would be good for the Migration Board to hire its own doctors to serve the interests of young people and the state in this matter. Thirdly, Madam President, it should be voluntary to undergo such an age assessment. But if someone refuses, in a case where age is judged unclear, that person should be judged as an adult. This does not apply, of course, if there are special reasons to refuse. The thesis that the one who has nothing to hide has nothing to lose from the truth should be applied. Fourthly, as part of providing these unaccompanied minors with the support they need, they have been placed in HVB homes, where the municipality is the principal. This means that the home workers daily see and meet these children, and the employees will get to know them after some time. Should there then be any doubt as to whether a child or an adult is involved, the municipality should have the right to request an age assessment in order for the children to feel safe. Finally, Madam President, I would like to mention that we Moderates believe that the new regulations should be applicable even if the application has been registered before 1 February 2017, which is the date proposed by the government.

It is untenable to have a system where resources intended for children are at risk of going to adults. Above all, it is untenable that, as we have just heard, children who are placed in accommodations in order to provide them with extra security are at risk of ending up with adults. These people are not only adults but have also shown that they have no respect for regulations and laws. It creates insecurity and uncertainty. The article from last week that had the title, yes, I'm 22... but in Sweden I am 17 years, and that's not a new story – it's not a new story. That is precisely why it is particularly serious that we have not taken concrete monitoring measures to this end. Our neighbours have been age-tested for several years, and it has been reported that up to 50, 60, 70 or sometimes 80 percent of those who have been tested for age have turned out to be adults. Of course, it should be pointed out that not everyone who says they are unaccompanied is being tested, but this happens when there is some uncertainty, but it is still a very high figure. Many have seen the opportunity and taken the opportunity to indicate a lower age, because they know that they then have a chance to be judged differently. It is also clear that one chooses the country that does not carry out thorough audits. When I was in Brussels three or four weeks ago at a conference on the asylum situation in Europe right now – about the migration flows here – I asked a representative of good men from the Netherlands about how they work with age assessments. Among other things, he replied that they do age assessments on and off. Now they have had a period where they have not had to do so, because the number of cases decreased drastically since they started to make age assessments. There were no longer as many people who gave the wrong age. This produced a preventive effect. It affects the flow of migration and how people behave – it affects whether or not they try to provide incorrect information. Now the Netherlands saw an increased need again and would therefore start with the age tests again. Legislation has an effect, as well as – unfortunately – our permissiveness. Madam President, I would like I said at the outset that this is a good first step, and it is precisely a first step. There is much more to be done than these small steps in the right direction, where at least a piece of legislation is put in place which shows more clearly that the burden of proof of facts giving particular advantages lies with the person who states that it is in a particular situation – in this case, that it is a minor. It is not the State that should prove that a person is an adult, but it is the State that claims that it is a minor and thus asserts the right to special benefits that should prove its need for this. Therefore, an offer is also introduced to those whose age is at risk of being written up by the Migration Agency. They can choose to be age - tested to prove that they are really minors. However, as this is designed in the proposal, as I said, it is only about those whose age the Migration Agency plans to write up – not about all cases where age is unclear. It may seem at first glance to be enough, for if it is unclear you will reasonably write down the age as the burden of proof is. However, given the stories and reports we have heard from the Migration Board last or last year, time pressure and a desire to close as many cases as quickly as possible and with as few appeals as possible is tending to give the asylum seeker a quick approval rather than a longer hearing. As a result, there is an obvious risk that we will end up in a similar situation as in 2015 with a very large inflow to Sweden and that, for simplicity's sake, the age will not be recorded because it will be a lengthy process. Rather, the person who claims to be a child is judged as a child in order to move on. Madam President, I would like Therefore, we in the Swedish Democrats believe that it should be made clear in the law that in all cases where it is not obvious that it is a child, one should assume that the person should be assessed as an adult, unless the person undergoes an age test that clearly shows that it is a child and unless it is unclear even after a medical age assessment, because medical age determinations are not always 100% safe. There will always be cases where the age of children or adults is unclear. Then we have a principle, benefit of the double, which means that you should judge in the person's favour. The person will therefore be registered as a child. It is a procedure that is nevertheless reasonable. If you have used all the possibilities available to see if it is a child or not and it is still unclear, it is reasonable not to risk a child being judged as an adult. However, the problem remains that adults may be able to live with children and thus put them at risk. It is therefore the view of the Swedish democrats that all cases where people are registered as children because, after a medical age assessment, one still does not know for sure should be distinguished from cases where it has actually been proven that they are children – for the sake of children's safety. The proposed legislation actually talks about the temporary nature of the decisions to be taken earlier in the asylum process, which is welcome. However, we Swedish democrats believe that they should be able to be final in that all the decisions to be taken should be based either on obviousness or on a medical assessment on a scientific basis, or that there is really strong evidence in other forms, such as approved and proven identity documents. This should apply, except in the few cases where age will still be unclear. We therefore believe that all decisions should be considered final from the outset. It is unreasonable that there should be new data later in the process that will change age, except for the unclear cases that have continued. These are the only cases that we believe should be subject to appeal. Among other things, the Moderates have a reservation that municipalities should be able to ask for age assessments. The Swedish Democrats do not support the reservation but propose a broad range of measures, including, as has been said, that everyone should be tested but also that they should check the age of those who have registered as unaccompanied minors since 2011, because that was when we got a much more generous interpretation of our migration legislation with changed practices. With our proposals, there would no longer be a need for municipalities themselves to request age assessments, but all unclear cases should nevertheless be taken up. On the other hand, we support the claim that if you refuse to participate in medical age assessment, even though it is really for the person's own good, it should be seen as an admission that the assessment that you are over 18 years old is actually correct and that you do not want to take the chance to prove that it is wrong. We also want to go one step further than the Moderates, who certainly propose to use the new legislation also for cases that have been registered before 1 February this year. We believe that all cases that the Migration Agency is still dealing with, which relate to people who have stated that they are children but where age is unclear, should also be covered by the legislation. In January, we were able to read several articles where the Migration Agency talks about 20 000 open cases with people who could be considered for age assessments. However, they are not covered by current legislation, as they are cases that were registered already in January of this year. The government claims in the bill that these people are excluded because they should already have reached such a stage in the process that the final decision after the end of the asylum examination is so close in time. But everyone who has been present in this country lately knows that the processing times at the Migration Board are extremely long. The time between the registration of an application and its completion is very long. Therefore, once again, all cases that are open are to be covered by the legislation.

i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-55	Madam President, I would like Age is of great importance in the Swedish asylum system and the judiciary, which is why it is important in many cases that we determine a person's age. Unfortunately, however, it is sometimes impossible to determine the exact age. People are not trees with tree rings, but we are quite different. The methods available to determine age are never perfect but provide answers within a certain range. The Left Party's criticism of age assessments has historically not been about the assessments themselves, but about the methods of assessment being so uncertain that it has led to a lack of legal certainty. This opinion is quite widespread and is shared by several organisations and experts, such as the Swedish Bar Association and the Swedish Children's Association. There are now more reliable methods and a clearer regulatory framework for the interpretation and implementation of assessments. The proposal we are debating today means that the rules should be interpreted to the benefit of the applicant in the event of uncertainty. This welcomes the Left Party. At the same time, there is always a risk that the assessment will be misleading. Therefore, medical assessments must be supplemented by other methods, such as an assessment of the child's psychosocial development and general maturity. There must also be the possibility of making exceptions for specific reasons, and the integrity of those being investigated must be carefully protected. The Government justifies the proposal that this should prevent adult asylum seekers from living together with unaccompanied minors on accommodation adapted for children and that resources allocated to children should not go to adult asylum seekers. The Left agrees that it is good that the proposal should address these problems. However, we are rather justifying the need for more legally certain age assessments by preventing the arbitrary revaluations of the age of unaccompanied minors, which could lead to the expulsion of children or the loss of their rights as children. One might also wonder why we talk so little about the importance of preventing children from being forced to live with adults if they are written up in age. Madam President, I would like Let's look back at what's happened. The methods of assessing the age of unaccompanied asylum seekers have varied over the years and have been done with more or less arbitrary methods. In a period around 2005 almost all of them were x-rayed, and the whole range of uncertainty, two to four years, was then used to the detriment of the asylum seeker. The introduction of new guidelines requiring that the uncertainty interval be to the advantage of the applicant effectively ended the age assessment. This resulted in problems with the placement of adults in youth housing. Then, instead, age assessments began to be made according to the administrators' own views, which of course led to doubtful assessments and widespread criticism. In 2012, the National Board of Health and Welfare issued new guidelines on how to use age assessments. Based on them, an agreement was made between the Migration Agency, the Swedish National Board of Health and Welfare and the Swedish Children's Association. It was decided that pediatricians would make the assessments and that X-rays would be only part of the basis. The age would be determined on the basis of an overall assessment based on the medical assessments and the child's psychosocial development and general maturity. It was the children's doctors who made the overall assessments, and they left only the overall assessments to the Migration Board. It was a good order. In 2015, the Migration Agency began to go around this agreement and request the X-rays from the overall assessment. However, X-rays are not very accurate because, as I said, there is a difference of two to four years, which is quite a lot. The Migration Board simply ignored the knowledge and expertise of children's doctors. Children's doctors refused to release the X-rays, and the Migration Board terminated the agreement. At present, both pediatricians and the Bar Association have decided not to contribute to age assessments, making it difficult for children to prove their actual age. In the debate, children's doctors have often been accused of slowing down the issue and aggravating the problem. The Left Party, too, has been claimed to be against age assessments, although it is the methods in themselves that we criticise. Among other things, criticism has been expressed by Minister of Migration Morgan Johansson – although it was in fact the Migration Agency, an authority for which the Minister of Migration is responsible, that broke the agreement. For some time now, relatively few investigations and age assessments have been carried out, with the exception of a large number of rapid age revaluations in connection with registration at the end of 2015. At the end of 2016, there was also a large amount of age revaluations, often on the day of the decision and without an investigation. At the same time, nearly half of the unaccompanied minors from Afghanistan who received a decision in 2016 received an age write-up. I think we all remember Systembolaget's massive campaign on age. We all know that we need to show ID until we're 25, precisely because it's very difficult by just looking at a human being to see if it's 171⁄2, 21 and so on. It's very different. What has happened at the rapid write-up is that the staff at the Migration Agency have looked and hipped and said: You are probably about that age. It is not a legal system in Sweden. On 5 March, the Migration Board issued a strong criticism of the age assessments made by the Swedish Agency in 2016. They announced that no more decisions that need to include an assessment of the asylum seeker's age will be taken until the new methods recommended by the National Board of Forensic Medicine are in place. The Finnish Immigration Service also announced that a special management team has been set up to strengthen quality and support the administrators and that extensive training efforts have been launched. Those who have received a decision and appealed it can request that the Migration Court ask the Migration Agency to order a medical age assessment of the National Board of Forensic Medicine. The Left Party very much welcomes the Migration Board's review of the age assessments they have carried out and the self-criticism authority expressed. However, in view of the seriousness of the errors detected, the variation in age assessment methods over the years and the far-reaching impact of the decisions on individuals, a more detailed analysis should be made of the way in which age assessments have been carried out over the years and the consequences they have had. Such an examination should aim to clarify the responsibility for the assessment and prevent similar situations from arising in the future. On Tuesday night last week, a plane of forced exiles took off to Kabul. We know there were people on the plane who were registered at age. In the light of everything I have said here – that there was not a reliable method of age assessment and that the Migration Agency has stopped age projections because they themselves saw shortcomings in legal certainty – it is clear that some unaccompanied persons may have been on the plane and ended up in a very serious situation. Sweden must not risk forced expulsion of children to one of the world's most dangerous countries. A government that claims to want to make the Convention on the Rights of the Child a law should recognise this. Therefore, the Left Party has supported the request for age assessment before enforcement put forward by the Center Party. In one month's time, when we debate the government proposal on residence permits for secondary school studies, we will debate the difficult circumstances under which unaccompanied minors live and what needs to be done further. I'm looking forward to that. Madam President, I would like As I said, it is very good that the government is putting forward a proposal to improve legal certainty in age assessments. It is also good that the bill clearly states that the principle of benefit of the double should apply and that the assessments in case of uncertainty should be interpreted to the advantage of the applicant and that the medical age assessments should be funded by the State and not the applicant. The Left Party also welcomes the fact that the assessment is made earlier in the process and that there will be an opportunity to appeal against the decisions, although the latter may be strengthened in some respects. The Left Party supports the proposal but, as I said, sees a few shortcomings that need to be addressed. We want the psychosocial assessment to be an important basis in cases where a medical age assessment cannot be carried out. It is important to be able to do this. Age assessment methods should therefore be complemented by an assessment of the child's psychosocial development and general maturity. With this, Madam President, I am in favour of reservation 4. I'm sorry I overwhelmed my time a little.

i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-100	Madam President, I would like The committee supports the Government's proposal for amendments to the Aliens Act, which requires age assessments of unaccompanied minors to be made earlier in the asylum process compared to today. I therefore support the committee's proposal in the Social Security Committee's report 18 and reject all reservations. In 2015, a record number of asylum seekers applied to Sweden – almost 163 000. We made every effort to achieve this, and we can be proud of the efforts made by Sweden in the autumn. But the situation finally became untenable. It was then also clear to us that the EU's common asylum policy was not working. The external borders were not maintained, and asylum seekers were allowed to choose for themselves which EU country to apply for asylum in. For Sweden, the refugee situation put the entire asylum system under great pressure. Other key social functions, such as health care, school and social care for children and young people, have also been heavily burdened. The large wave of refugees in autumn 2015 made it clear that our model to carry out age assessment later in the asylum process does not work. This leads, among other things, to many asylum seekers, who are later considered adults, being treated as unaccompanied minors during the asylum process. Apart from having a huge cost to society, where resources for children instead go to adults, it is not good for adults to live with children at HVB homes or support homes. It is unfortunate that adult asylum seekers may be treated as children during the asylum process and thus benefit from the rights and benefits intended for children. This may include placement in particular housing, schooling and health care. In order for the proposal to have the desired effect, an assessment of the applicant's age is required as early as possible. This means that the Migration Agency must investigate the question of the applicant's age more carefully in the initial oral investigation and, where appropriate, give the applicant reasonable advice to consider the question of consent to a medical age assessment. However, where the Authority offers the applicant to undergo a medical age assessment, the refusal of consent without acceptable reasons should lead to the Authority's preliminary assessment, i.e. that the applicant has not made it likely that he or she is under 18 years of age. The Government proposes in the bill that the Migration Agency should assess an alien's age earlier in the asylum process than is being done today. If there is uncertainty as to whether or not an asylum seeker who claims to be an unaccompanied minor is under 18 years of age, the Finnish Immigration Service shall, as soon as possible, carry out an age assessment and take a temporary decision on the age of the applicant. Before a temporary decision is made which means that the applicant is deemed to be 18 years of age or older, the Finnish Immigration Service shall give the applicant the opportunity to undergo a medical age assessment. The Government also proposes that the Finnish Immigration Service's temporary decision be subject to appeal. Madam President, I would like We Social Democrats believe that this is a long-awaited change, which can now become a reality as early as 1 May if Parliament agrees with the proposal in the report. I therefore call again for rejection of all twelve reservations and for approval of the proposal contained in the report.

i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-141	Madam President, I would like I am in favour of Amendment No 4, and otherwise I support the committee's proposal in the report. It is clear that when we are now debating road safety in this House, our thoughts go to those affected by the bus accident this weekend. Of course, we think of the relatives, parents, friends, students and all who were affected. It gives us thought: life is fragile. We, as politicians, must do our utmost to prevent this type of accident from occurring. I would like to say that we have a good basis on which to stand: we have the zero vision. I can have some respect for the fact that, as an opposition, you want the parliament to make an announcement and ask the government to come back with things – if you have something concrete to suggest. But I do not see that there is any such thing in what the majority is proposing. You want a strategy for safer roads. Let's back off the tape and see what the zero vision is. Zero vision is a completely unique and, I would say, very Swedish creation. It is a product of a collaboration where researchers look at causation: What is actually to be done to prevent people from getting killed in traffic? It is also an effect of cooperation where the people's movement has been actively involved. NTF, sobriety organisations, MHF and several others have participated in the zero vision work. We politicians in this House have decided that the zero vision should exist and that we are prepared to make the legislative changes needed to make it a reality and to make the investments needed to make the roads safe. We are prepared to ensure that the right measures are taken. It is, of course, also about the state authorities. This applies not only to the Swedish Transport Administration, or the Swedish Road Administration at the time, but also to the Swedish National Police Board. Zerovision is a unique collaboration between science, peoples' movements, politics, actors, i.e. Sweden's Åkeri company, taxi and the state authorities. By doing so, we have been able to achieve that in Sweden, compared to other countries, there is little risk of being harmed when you are on the road. We should not be content with this. The target we have set for a maximum of 220 deaths by 2020 requires renewed efforts. But then you'll have to see what's effective. We argue on the part of the majority that we should be fairly careful of the roads. Taking care of roads is not just about spending money on maintenance, it is about ensuring that the roads are not damaged. Then you'll see what breaks down a road. It is not passenger cars, it is not bicycles, it is not mopeds and it is actually very small buses. It's about heavier trucks. We need heavier trucks in this country to transport forest products, but we are concerned by the cooperation government that they should not be used when transferring to other modes of transport is possible or where there is a risk of damaging the road. This is done in areas where there are frost damage. This is not so much true in the country I come from, but in large parts of the country the road is fragile when the temperature commutes between plus and minus. Then you should not have too heavy vehicles on the road, because then you risk getting the pits that can cause accidents for others. It is also a question of controlling vehicles. There have been rather worrying signals that the situation of the employees and the maintenance of buses has been pushed so that one may not be as sure as before that things are being handled as they should. I do not intend to anticipate the investigations in any way, because I have no insight into this. I assume that the accident investigations that are being carried out will tell us what the reason was in the present case. I remember when I was new to the Transport Committee. At the time, there was a party here in the Riksdag that wanted to remove the seatbelts, because they thought it was a restriction of freedom to wear a seatbelt when you go. One may think that it is a restriction of freedom, but it is also a fairly large restriction of freedom to get rid of one's life. We are concerned, and it is part of the zero vision, that the protective equipment to be provided to protect both those in the vehicle and others should be used. Here we have a factor that I did not mention before but which is important in order to explain why Sweden compared to other countries has a fairly safe traffic. It's about the automotive industry. They have been active in developing and taking the necessary protective measures. Let us now look to the future, because the subject here is not some kind of history lecture. What else can we do? What are we going to do to reach the target of a maximum of 220 deaths by 2020? And what are we going to set for the future? The overall objective of traffic has been changed to include it in the role of traffic to contribute to health. It is an urgent objective to contribute to increased health and to postpone or avoid public diseases. It is a scourge for all the people affected, but it is also a serious obstacle to us who want to carry out political reforms in this House that we have problems with sick leave and that people do not feel well. I recently wrote an article together with a researcher in Helsingborg's Dagblad that shows that at least 30 minutes of pulse-enhancing activity per day is a pretty good way to try to postpone or reduce the risk of suffering from public diseases. How do you do that? A natural way is to cycle to and from the place you are going to – either school or work. I believe that we should intertwin the work for road safety and to reduce injuries with the work on improving health. Of course, we should have protective equipment and other things, but we should not have anything that hampers people's active mobility and makes them drive or travel if the route makes the choice realistic. What does this government do? We have a reservation on the part of the Cooperation Government saying that there is no basis for an announcement. What the government has done is to initiate an investigation into reduced base rates in urban areas. The basic speeds are important for people to dare to move to active mobility. There are still cities where parents do not want the children to get around because they fear that the children will be hit by motorists driving too fast. It is therefore important that we can agree to lower the speed of the bike so that the choice between the bike and the car becomes easier and feels safer for those who perform it. We have looked and turned and turned the issue of alco-boms. I do not think there is any difference among us in committee, but we agree that there should be no alcohol in your blood when you get behind the wheel of a heavy vehicle. I think we are all just as eager to make sure that this thing is settled once and for all before there is any new accident. We are interested in looking at how to use communication equipment. It can be said that this is one of the few areas of road safety where Sweden does not stand up very well in competition with other countries. I believe that only Russia has an equally liberal approach to the extent to which the driver himself chooses whether to do texting and other things. Certainly there has been a slight tightening up so that you can hold people to account if it is possible to prove negligence, but according to the experience I have gained from the Police Authority this evidence is quite difficult. It is probably time that we take this one step further and perhaps do as has been done in other countries where drivers simply have to drive aside if they are to engage in communication equipment that can draw attention from the steering wheel. I think we have a good chance of moving forward in this. I would also like to remind you that the speed is extremely important both for the occurrence of accidents and for preventing accidents from having a fatal or serious outcome. The introduction of automatic speed monitoring is one of the major reasons why zero vision has been successful. Earlier they thought: Build safer roads. The announcement has a scent of the discussion from the last century that if you build safer roads, it all solves itself. However, when you look at the causes of accidents, you see that the speed in which the vehicle is being driven is crucial, partly because of an accident and partly because of the consequences. What was at first incredibly controversial, that is to say that the speed of the camera was monitored and that it was possible to prevent accidents – the aim was not to bring down as many people as possible, but to keep the speed limits on the signs – has been a very cost-effective investment. As a small side matter, I can say that it has been a cost-effective investment not only to reduce the risk of accidents but also to reduce the climate impact of traffic. After all, the speed is directly proportional to the amount of CO2 emitted when the vehicle is being driven. As I understand it, the Swedish Transport Administration and the Police Authority are building this system now, so we may not need any further initiatives on our part. But to understand why the zero vision is something that others are interested in – foreign delegations come here to study this – one must understand this with ATK. Another thing is the rebuilding of roads to two-plus-one with centre wire. This has led to irritation in many places – I do not know how many emails, letters and phone calls I have received in connection with this – but when you look at the research it is easy to note that you can make a stretch of meeting free you reduce the risk of accidents occurring, especially fatal accidents. It may limit speed, but it is not certain that it is necessarily a negative thing. Having two-plus-one with middle wire can make it easier for people to keep a signposted speed. I think it must be realised that, although one may sometimes be in a hurry somewhere and be annoyed by not getting there fast enough, it is our very firm view that one should be able to travel safely on the roads. No one should have to receive such information as the relatives received this week. I think that we should work together here in Parliament.

Madam President, I would like We support all of our reservations and also our specific opinion. But for time winning, I only ask for reservations 6 and 19. I would like to begin by telling you about our reservation 7, which deals with the speed of our roads. Since the last government decided in 2009 to change the speed limits, so that even 40, 60, 80 and on our motorways 120 kilometres an hour would be allowed, the Finnish Transport Agency has implemented many speed reductions of 10 and sometimes 20 kilometres an hour, while the increases have been very few. I would like to emphasise strongly that speed limits should be set solely on the basis of road safety aspects. Furthermore, if other reasons are used, confidence in the speed limits will disappear. We must note that this has unfortunately been the case. The Finnish Transport Agency wants 80 percent of motorists to respect all speed limits. That is not the case today. On several roads, speeds have been reduced too much, not least in northern Sweden. In the past, it was 90 kilometres an hour on most stretches in northern Sweden. These are straight and narrow roads. Now the Swedish Transport Administration has reduced to 80 kilometres per hour. As a result, motorists and motorcyclists lose respect for current speed signs. In the hall of driving 90 kilometers per hour, many people today ignore 80 and instead drive 100 - 110. You have to respect the speed limit that applies on a road. I can take an example from the areas where I live, namely E6 between Trelleborg and Malmö. In the past, the speed limit was lowered from 110 kilometres per hour to 90 kilometres per hour the last two kilometres into Malmö. Today, the speed limit is reduced to 60 kilometers per hour — on a four - lane highway. As a result, motorists who in the past lowered their speed from 110 to 90 kilometres per hour today do not care to reduce their speed at all. You blow in towards Malmö at 110 kilometers an hour where it says 60, because you have lost respect for the speed signs. Moreover, since today we have a situation where there is a greater chance of seeing a golden eagle in the sky than a traffic police officer on the road, many people do not care about speed limits. The only people who are currently caught in a speed check are basically those who get caught in a speed camera. These speed cameras are also educationally signposted a few hundred meters before. If you get caught in a speed camera, you're pretty screwed up, or you're sitting with your mobile phone and sending text messages or playing Wordfeud. Our reservation 2 is that we want it to be forbidden to drive if a stagnant school shot picks up or leaves a child. I would like to draw attention to the fact that fatal accidents and serious injuries involving children occur all too often in connection with boarding and boarding from school shootings. It is clear that the current warning system with yellow flashing lights on the school pushers does not have the desired effect. Many testify to the fact that there are motorists who pass school shootings at too high a speed. Instead, replacing the yellow flashing light with a red fixed light and a statutory ban would minimise the risk of fatal accidents and damage associated with embarkation. For example, a ban on passing school shootings that have stopped for boarding or boarding has been introduced in the United States. The results are reported to be very good. I believe that the government should therefore ban the re-run of stagnant school shootings. The Traffic Regulation 3 Chapter. Paragraph 15 says:aside from other cases specified in particular, drivers must maintain a sufficiently low speed... when the vehicle approaches... a school shot. What is a sufficiently low speed, Madam President? Let me give you an example. When you invite friends to your home for dinner in the evening and the friends are going home again, you often say: Drive home carefully! It's a little bit to insure itself... in case something happens, it's not even your own fault. If the friends drive away and come home safely, everything is peace and joy. If the brothers drive in the ditch and injure themselves, you can think: But I told them to drive home carefully! Yet they hurt themselves! The same is true when it comes to passing school shootings. The law states that the driver should maintain a sufficiently low speed in the circumstances. If the speed limit is 90 kilometers per hour, the driver may think it is low enough to pass a school shot at 50 kilometers per hour. That motorist can't be convicted of that. Only if the driver drives on a school child can it be observed that the driver did not keep the speed of the car low enough. That is why I think there must be a change of law. If there is a stationary school shooting, it shall be red light and unauthorised to pass this school shooting. Our reservation 21 is about banning moped and bike driving on two-plus-one roads. Today there are many so-called two-plus-one paths. This means that the roads have been divided into three lanes, one of which alternately belongs to one direction or another. Many of these roads are common highways that have been rebuilt. The third file has been added at the expense of the road user, the same road that mopeds and cyclists usually use to facilitate faster traffic. However, two-plus-one roads are dangerous for cyclists and mopedists. This is because they have been referred to a generally very narrow, sometimes almost non-existent, road user on these motorways. It's as thin as 20 centimeters. Earlier on these roads there were broad roadsides, which provided some shelter for the two-wheelers. Now that space has been shrunk to a few inches. It's slimmer than a bike handler. Those who continue to use the road thereby expose themselves to an extremely high risk of injury. Of course, passing a few decimetres by motor traffic at 100 kilometres per hour is contrary to every syllable in the zero vision. Paradoxically, cycling and moped skiing is prohibited on motorways and motorways, where the road is usually one and a half meters wide. For a cyclist or mopedist it is therefore safer to cycle and drive a moped on a motorway, where it is forbidden, than on a two-plus-one road. i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-398	Mr President, I would like to Today we are faced with a proposal from the government. It is supported by the Alliance, as this is part of the government's and the Alliance's agreement on migration policy. The proposal concerns fixed-term building permits. This proposal will allow the need to listen to public interests to be circumvented. It can also create many new exclusion areas. The fixed-term building permits covered by this proposal mean that you can have a building set up for 15 years. The law is to be applied for a period of six years, which means that these temporary buildings can remain until 2038. Words are used as fixed-term building permits, but here we are talking about a very long time to come. The fixed-term building permits proposed in the bill also mean that a variety of requirements currently imposed on permanent housing, such as quality, public interest in new construction and the integration of these homes into the environment, can be waived. These demands can be completely waived if this proposal becomes a reality. The consequences of the proposal would be substantial, and the proposal is very far-reaching for society in the future. Among other things, long-term housing and community planning would be destroyed – in future construction, dwellings built with fixed-term building permits could stand in the way of long-term housing planning, as they can last for up to 15 years. We are talking about a very long period of time. It is believed that this should be one of the urgent measures to solve the housing shortage. However, this may mean that we are reducing the long-term possibility of building more homes, as these temporary dwellings will end up as a brake block for future construction. What the government and the Alliance think should be a solution to obtaining housing may be an improvement over a very short period of time, but in the long term this can put obstacles in the way of increased housing construction. In 2016, the Riksdag decided that municipalities must accept a number of refugees – a kind of distribution ratio across the country. This so-called instruction law, combined with the fixed-term building permits, may oblige municipalities to build – or set up – a variety of temporary dwellings. In the long run, there is a risk that this will create more exclusion areas – more vulnerable areas. I wonder what response we had received from the police if this proposal had gone there for a referral. Temporary housing poses a great risk. In the long run, these could be very large areas that could become socially vulnerable. Those who own these homes will have little interest in managing either them or the environment to any great extent, as it is temporary housing. What is meant to create better conditions for obtaining more housing will thus perhaps, in the long run, create huge problems with new alienation areas. Furthermore, this means disregarding the public interest. This way a building can be built near your own residential area or near your own house without being able to influence it. The government creates such an opening through this proposal, which is therefore very far-reaching in many ways. We know that many other laws that have been considered temporary have turned into permanent ones. When the housing that can be set up over a 15-year period is then to be moved or removed from the place where it stood, we may end up in a situation where a new proposal is put forward to allow these homes to remain. This would mean that the interest of neighbours in influencing their environment would be completely eliminated, since 15 years ago it was possible to build housing in connection with other homes, without the neighbours being able to participate, and that these dwellings would be permanent after 15 years. This also makes it possible to build business-related housing. We know that a great deal can happen over a 15-year period – companies are expanding and new companies want access to industrial land, but housing has been built there. In particular, we wanted to improve the conditions for young researchers. We wanted to reduce the number of fixed-term contracts in academia and enable clearer career paths. We felt that gender equality should be intensified, including through an increased proportion of female professors, and that research funding should be distributed more equally. We argued for the importance of strengthening the school's scientific base through more practice-related school research. We argued that humanistic and social science research should be strengthened. We wanted to see an improved research link to basic education, a better research mobility and an increased internationalisation. This is now in the bill, and I am proud of that. All universities throughout the country need a strong research base. We therefore criticised the fact that the bourgeois government disadvantaged the four young universities and the country's colleges in the allocation of research resources. I'm glad the government is now giving a new signal. Among other things, the basic resource for research will be increased to at least 12 000 SEK per student. The other individual educational providers, who were denied research resources by the bourgeois government, will now also have a basic resource for research and education at doctoral level. All universities and colleges need to be strengthened. It is therefore welcome that the government is now proposing that the governance and resource allocation system be reviewed. It was an important issue for us when we were in opposition. Increased collaboration between academia, industry and the public sector is vital. That perspective was toned down in the bourgeois research bill that came in 2008. It got better in the research bill 2012, but now we take out the step length for an increased collaboration. The title of the research bill indicates this position shift: Knowledge in collaboration – for society's challenges and increased competitiveness. In this context, we can note that the Government states that collaboration should also be financially rewarded and used together with current quality indicators, such as publications and citations, as well as external funds when new funding is to be allocated. Mr President, I would like to Today we are facing major societal challenges. The Research Bill sets out three major global societal challenges and two national societal challenges. The global challenges are the climate challenge, the health challenge and the digitalisation challenge. The two national challenges are safe, inclusive and sustainable societies as well as improved learning outcomes in the school and education system. The six ten-year national research programmes of the Research Bill will be important instruments for meeting these challenges. They should be interdisciplinary and cross-sectoral and integrate education and research. In this way, we enable coherent knowledge environments. Added to this is another powerful instrument to meet societal challenges. This is the proposal for a power pool for innovation – Testbed Sweden. Here we find the five collaboration programmes of the National Innovation Council, which further strengthens collaboration. This initiative significantly reinforces strategic innovation areas. The investments in industrial research institutes and test and demonstration environments are also important to strengthen Swedish innovation capacity. To this will also be added that the strategic research areas remain and will further strengthen the focus of the research proposal. The report contains an announcement from five opposition parties on the labour market link of higher education. Like many of the reservations, the announcement has the character of breaking open doors. The joint reservation from S, MP and V states that the government also thinks this is important, as is also stated in the research bill. The government is working on this, so the announcement is more of a blow to the air. I would also like to say a few words about the infrastructure of research. Often we talk about ESS, MAX IV and Sci Life Lab. They are of course very important, but there are more infrastructure investments that are important for research and innovation. I know that there is concern about long-term financing. Significant amounts have been added to the higher education institutions in the two previous proposals, and this is also the case in this proposal. It should provide an increased possibility of financing. At the same time, the Government states in the research bill that it intends to review the issue of the possibility of charging fees from those who use research infrastructures. I would like to stress the importance of ensuring the long-term financing of research infrastructure. Mr President, I would like to Free and independent research is extremely important. It's supposed to be protected. This is not in contrast to the importance of increased collaboration between academia, industry and the public sector. We need both a strong curiosity research and a strong challenge-driven research. I would like to conclude by saying that the research will be in the service of education. Through increased knowledge and new insights, we gain a deeper understanding of ourselves and the world in which we live. Research is a tool for free thought and critical voice. We need fearless and committed researchers who challenge our way of thinking and acting. In this way, research becomes a defence for democracy and a tool for progressive social development. Mr President, I would like to I therefore support the committee's proposal in the report.

The national research programmes include: the climate programme, sustainable society building, migration and integration, antibiotic resistance, applied welfare research, and work life research. There is much to do to address inequality at Swedish universities and colleges. Students choose places based on stereotypical gender roles. Professors are mostly men and are easier to advance in positions. Research funds are allocated to men's excellence centres. Of strategic research funds, 87.3 per cent are allocated to men, while women receive 12.7 per cent. Their report shows that not only the strategic research funds but also the basic grants are very unequally distributed. Men are awarded 61% of the funding, but only 58% of the researchers. So it is only 3 percentage points that differ, which may sound like surface python money, but that is not the case. That is more than SEK 80 million. Much of the explanation for this is that basic funds are often used to finance salaries for professors, where men are clearly over-represented. Universities and higher education institutions must overcome the misallocation of funding. Research can no longer be a man-dominated world. In its proposal, the Government proposes a number of important measures to address serious gender equality problems, including concrete targets for the recruitment of female professors and sharp demands for an equal distribution of research funding. The Left Party, as we often reason, wants a little more. We want to tighten the requirements further. We want the government to investigate the introduction of a goal that no more than half of all government-funded research funding should go to men who do research. A researcher's work suffers if the person concerned has to worry about his or her own financial situation. At a workplace with many professions, the working environment is not good if some constantly go around worrying about their position. It is therefore gratifying that the Government's proposal now proposes that the grant be removed. This is an important step towards better job security for doctoral students, and that is the right way to go. Possible external obstacles for people to work with what they want to be countered. Today, knowledge about the accessibility of researchers is too poor. i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-755	Madam President, I would like I shall begin by saying that we naturally agree with our reservations, but I only support alliance reservations 2 and 10. Thomas Strand was very clear about the government's goal with the new bill. The Government's new goal is for Sweden to be one of the world's leading research and innovation countries and a leading knowledge nation, where high-quality research, higher education and innovation lead to society's development and welfare and business competitiveness and respond to the societal challenges we face, both in Sweden and globally. The Government's ambition, i.e. that Sweden should be one of the leading knowledge nations, is of course very good. Sweden's future lies in being a knowledge nation, where innovations not only create a better world but help us create more and new businesses and new jobs. For this to be achieved, the whole policy needs to be linked, not only to this bill, but also to other parts of the policy – everything from research and development to how we manage our entrepreneurs in terms of taxes, demands and support to develop. Here I mean, for example, the business environment we create in Sweden: the right of deduction for research and development and for investments, labour costs, corporate taxation and labour supply. We need more companies that start, develop and stay in Sweden. Collaboration between society and business provides an increased opportunity to ensure that more new businesses are started through innovation. Sweden needs a long-term sustainable strategy for research and innovation in order to maintain a strong position in international competition. It requires clear objectives, which can be followed up, and strong leadership. We can all say that it is not best at anything by writing it in a document. Funds need to be added, targets need to be followed up, and you need to keep on making sure that you are on the right track. The government's ambition is high and should be, but leadership that steers towards the goal must include control stations and clear targets so that you can see if you are on the right track. There is a need not only for horizontal objectives but also for milestones that can be reconciled as and when. The Alliance therefore requires the introduction of milestones, which are measurable. One of the proposals for measurable targets is that Sweden's share of allocated funds from the EU's research and partnership programmes should be at least 4.2 per cent by 2020. We believe that these are quite clear objectives. You always have to be able to see if you're on the right track. An overall objective that spans quite a long time can be achieved if you do not have control stations very far away. I also believe that Sweden is dependent on the creation of new businesses and new jobs. Many of the companies created can be based on innovation. In an international comparison, however, we are very poor at commercialising innovation. There are examples out there that show that other countries are much better at this. I think you could learn from them and actually get help as a nation. In the bill, the government does not present a ready-made model for the distribution of future research funds. It does not define, as several speakers have mentioned, the indicator of interaction, but it wants to give that indicator the same weight as the indicators currently in force: external grants, citations and publications. State aid should go to the establishment of geriatric centres in three regions. The task of these centres should be to provide the conditions for clinical research, education, care and rehabilitation, as well as cooperation between municipalities, primary care and inpatient care. i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-771	Madam President, I would like An important starting point for research policy, for both the government and opposition parties, is to protect free research. There is a basic consensus. It is reflected in the need to strengthen the long-term strategic planning of universities and higher education institutions through increased so-called basic funding and the need for research policies to respond to global and national societal challenges. These are challenges such as climate and environment, health, increased digitalisation and a socially and environmentally sustainable society, and, with the help of research, to achieve improved knowledge results throughout the Swedish school and education system. Research policy benefits from long-term and political consensus. Anyone who reads the report of the Committee on Education can then see that there are certainly a large number of reservations. On the other hand, on the other hand, the accurate reader can quickly conclude that, although there is a difference in individualities and nuances, there is basically agreement on the overall direction of policy. That's good. Research policy is ill-suited as an ideological battlefield for democratic parties. What is ultimately at stake is safeguarding the scientific foundation of our common society. This foundation is extremely important for the functioning of democracy. It is about trying, without political intentions, to direct or influence what is facts and what is fiction and still to rely, regardless of the values underlying the policy you wish to pursue, on the methodology of scientific knowledge collection to describe reality as truthfully as possible. It is about distinguishing between facts and opinions and distinguishing between what we actually know and what we may feel and experience at the moment. In today's debate we are increasingly talking about factual resistance and the new possibilities which technological development has once again opened up to unrefined propaganda, a propaganda in which lies are regularly used as weapons. But let us remember that this is not new for our very time. History shows us many examples where development has taken a devastating course contrary to reason and better knowledge. I sincerely hope that the sanctuary that science has found in the democracies of the world over the last 100 years is not a temporary parenthesis in human history, but rather a legacy that will be passed on step by step to success in all corners of the world, as well as that our knowledge societies do not turn into pure emotional societies. It bodes well that we here in the Swedish Parliament have a fundamental consensus over block and party boundaries both on the importance of free and independent research and on the importance of taking on society's challenges with the help of science. Mr President, I would like to Sweden has a globally high proportion of high-cited publications. The share has increased slightly in recent years, but several other countries have experienced larger increases. Furthermore, Sweden, as we have heard in the debate, has significantly increased its public funds for research and development through the two preceding research policy proposals. The purpose of this proposal is to ensure that the large public funds that are added to the research are also used in a way that will increase the quality of Swedish research, innovation and higher education in the long term. Increased so-called basic funding for our universities is important and desirable. These funds shall be provided in accordance with the principles applicable to free research and shall not be directed towards their orientation. However, the government will have expectations of the higher education institutions about what will develop and become better, and we will follow it up. These include increased gender equality in research, attractive career paths, increased mobility and open competition for services, increased research links in higher education, higher education institutions' responsibility for research evaluation systems that can be linked to a national evaluation of research, increased collaboration with the surrounding society in research and, last but not least, increased quality in research. It is therefore important that these expectations are also clearly followed up and evaluated. In addition to continuous government management, there should be national monitoring and evaluation systems. The government has great confidence in the ability of universities to take responsibility for their quality work. However, in order to achieve increased quality throughout the system, Swedish research needs to be monitored and evaluated at national level. The Government therefore proposes that UKÄ, the University Chancellor's Office, be given a new assignment. The University Chancellor's Office is currently responsible for quality assurance of higher education and for supervision and follow-up of the university's activities. In order to achieve a more coherent system, the Government intends to entrust the Authority with the task of ensuring quality assurance in research as well. A more coherent system of quality assurance, which includes the entire university's operations, contributes to an increased link between research and education, for example through a comprehensive review of the entire knowledge environments. Mr President, I would like to Some of the increased basic funding is expected to go towards funding the infrastructure of research. Both the Swedish Research Council and the universities need to make clear priorities of which initiatives are most urgent in the future. The previous initiatives on ESS and Max IV are long-term and remain. It is the Government's objective that Sweden should get a higher exchange on these extensive investments. In order to increase and promote the use of our large-scale infrastructures such as ESS, Max IV and Sci Life Lab by Swedish researchers and industry, special funding is given to the Swedish Research Council. The establishment of ESS means that Sweden is hosting one of the biggest investments in European research infrastructure. As part of developing the Swedish hostship, the Ministry of Education has appointed a coordinator to assist the Government Office in improving coordination of efforts around ESS. Mr President, I would like to It is sometimes easy for us to get caught up in discussions about the systems and the infrastructure of research. But we must not forget the individuals. It is with them that the ideas and the driving forces exist. They are our knowledge developers and bearers. A young researcher today can go many years without having a secure job. After a doctoral degree, it is often crucial that there are attractive and clear career paths for prominent young researchers to choose a continued career within the university. Otherwise, there is a risk that many, not least women, will opt out of a career in academia. It is therefore important that fixed-term employment within the university is reduced and that it becomes clearer what applies in order to qualify for an open-ended employment. At the same time, it is important that mobility among the university's teaching and research staff is increased. In order to create a clearer career structure and at the same time promote mobility, merit employment needs to be more uniform nationally regulated and advertised in open competition, nationally and, as far as possible, internationally. The Government's ambition is to review the Higher Education Ordinance so that merit employment can lead to a right to be tried for permanent employment. Although the proportion of women has been higher than the proportion of men in higher education for a long time and the proportion of women is close to half among senior lecturers, only 26 per cent of professors are women today in 2017. The Government concludes that for the 2017 – 2019 target period ambitious targets are needed to increase the proportion of women becoming professors. The Government believes that 50% of newly recruited professors should be women by 2030. In addition, all government agencies that finance research and development are tasked with integrating a gender perspective in the distribution of research funding. The importance of equal distribution of research funding should be highlighted in the existing mandates of universities on gender mainstreaming. Mr President, I would like to Finally, I would like to emphasise that the concept of collaboration runs like a red thread through this bill, which also explains the title of the bill. For the government, it is a high priority that universities' collaboration with the surrounding society is developed. Increased collaboration has the potential to promote quality development for both education and research, and to contribute to business growth as well as to develop the public sector and civil society. Allowing co-financing to influence the allocation of increased basic grants is one of several governance tools that the government intends to use. One guiding insight is that a small, export-dependent country like Sweden needs to have a functioning and well-developed strategic collaboration between all actors, which can contribute to Sweden's success in international competition as one of the world's leading research and innovation countries and a leading knowledge nation.

i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-877	Mr President, I would like to This debate is more appropriate in a security debate, but as Valter Mutts and my motion for the establishment of a peace institute in Gotland are being dealt with, and unfortunately rejected, in the report now under debate, we are now having to take it. Why is a peace institute needed, and why on Gotland? A good rule in all contexts is to think about what problem you want to solve and then think about suitable solutions. Unfortunately, however, this does not seem to be the case too often when it comes to security policy. As soon as human security comes up, the spinal reflex of too many seems to be calling for military armaments and more weapons, regardless of the security problem to be tackled. It may be climate change or cyber threats, hacker attacks and the like. Let me take an example of all the major security problems facing the world and Sweden. The media have reported over the last few days about the lack of groundwater, which unfortunately seems to be getting worse and will get worse with climate change. Most of us in Sweden, with the exception of those on Öland and Gotland, have been favored, even spoiled with abundant supply of clean water. Many of us have taken it for granted while drought and water shortages have long plagued people in large parts of the world. But how ever could we ever get more drinking water by military means? Sometimes it becomes purely counterproductive and, I'm sorry, even stupid. Again, the example of water: How can anyone even think about allowing shooting exercises in Vättern, one of the country's most important drinking water supplies, or for that matter a giant open pit, a mine, in its immediate vicinity? But more shooting exercises are called for from some places when the world is uncertain. One explanation for the obsession with military armament is, of course, the powerful special interests that have great money to gain from military armament. Already Dwight D Eisenhower warned in his farewell speech as U.S. President in 1961 about the military industrial complex, that is, the close, symbiotic cooperation between the arms industry, the military and the political leadership, in the case of the U.S. Congress – a kind of cartel that works against the public interest with economic gain as motivation. But we elected representatives in the Riksdag have a duty to think for themselves and listen broadly. We have no excuse whatsoever to fall into any form of functional stupidity – a concept coined by researcher Mats Alvesson in Lund. It is about the absence of critical reflection, a state of unity and consensus that makes the employees of an organization avoid questioning decisions, structures and visions. We simply need much more research and dissemination on non-violence methods and peaceful conflict resolution in order to create and maintain peace and to resolve conflicts. A little further north in the Baltic Sea is a good example to be inspired by, namely the Åland Islands Peace Institute. It works both in practice and with research on peace and conflict issues in a broad sense and based on Åland's special position as a demilitarised area. The Institute focuses on autonomy, minority issues, demilitarisation and conflict management. I warmly recommend that you google and read a little more about what you do there. Another role model for a peace institute on Gotland is Albert Einstein Institution in Boston, USA, founded by Dr Gene Sharp in 1983. Dr. Sharp has been called the Clausewitz of non-violent Clausewitz. For many years he has been researching, developing and formulating non-violence methods and strategies in the fight against dictatorships, war, genocide and oppression, for peace and democracy. His books and writings have been widely distributed and have come to great benefit in freedom empires in widely differing countries. Dr. Sharp's ideas and knowledge have rightly received great attention, and interest is increasing. Only in 2015 has the organisation conducted training and consultations with groups from more than 13 countries, including Turkey, Vietnam, Burundi, Venezuela, Eritrea and Ethiopia. They have met activists as well as those in power, and in addition there are translations of writings into six new languages and extensive activity on social media. Reporting on non-violent actions is important, particularly as they are too often not addressed in the media. Dr. Sharp was rewarded for his long-standing work for peace and democracy with the Right Livelihood Award 2012 here in Parliament. It is highly recommended that you google when we are done here today. Non-violence methods have proved far more effective than violent methods in the fight for democracy and human rights. I'll give you another reading tip, the third. There are two American scientists, Erica Chenoweth and Maria J. Stephan, who has studied 323 major political uprisings between 1900 and 2006. They have concluded that those based on non-violence have been about twice as successful as those involving violence. They believe that this can be explained, among other things, by the fact that far more people are prepared to resist by non-violence methods than by resorting to violence. Of course, it is also less likely that non-violent acts will provoke violent counter-reactions from a superior apparatus of violence. A non-violent movement also builds a more stable ideological and organisational basis for future democratic development than a violent movement, which risks leading to the replacement of one oppressive regime with just another. In view of this, it seems not only morally but also economically unreasonable to invest so little in conflict resolution and peace-building by non-violence methods, while huge sums of money are allocated to military armament. This applies globally, but it also applies in Sweden. Good work is being done. We have Sipri, the Peace Diplomats, Sida and many actors who do good things in Sweden, but it is small money compared to what is invested in weapons. It's dangerous in the world, more weapons, arm up! It's not enough. It's not good enough. We can't do that. We have to think for ourselves. One way to correct this imbalance – that peace is so shamefully underfunded while the war is just as shamefully overfunded – would be to approve Valter Mutts and my motion to establish a peace institution on Gotland and thereby strengthen the Baltic Sea as a sea of peace. It would also have a great symbolic value. I hope there will be approval on another occasion. I'm not asking for your approval now, but I've delivered the message.

However, the study considers that consumer problems related to telephone sales are of such a scale and nature that action is needed, precisely to strengthen consumer protection. According to the assessment of the investigation, it is important that the Swedish Consumer Agency prioritises infringements related to telephone sales and that the authority prepares procedures to meet specific difficulties that may occur in these cases. The investigation also proposes to introduce a written requirement for telephone sales to consumers. During the journey there was also an amendment to the law on 1 October 2016. It strengthened the scope of sanctions for the Consumer Ombudsman, who can now, in some cases, directly prohibit traders from continuing with a particular marketing measure. This is positive, but more needs to be done. One thing we all thought would remedy the problem of unsolicited telephone sales to private individuals was the Nix register. It was thought that would be the solution to avoid unwanted salesmen. But it turns out it doesn't help. In a letter to the Government earlier this year, the Swedish Consumer Agency has said that the protection against unwanted telephone sales that the Nix register was intended to provide has been increasingly eroded. The Swedish Consumer Agency has now terminated the industry agreement on rules for the register and makes the assessment that problems with telephone sales are of such a nature and scope that other measures are required. The Swedish Consumer Agency believes that the written confirmation that the investigation also proposes would ensure that the consumer is given better opportunity to know what to do before committing to a contract. The requirement for written confirmation would in principle eliminate the risk of consumers ending up in binding contracts that they have not intended to enter into. In addition to strengthening consumer protection to a large extent, such a regime would increase consumer confidence in the sales channel as such. Anyone who has taken part in written contracts when buying goods knows that they can be both long and hard to read. It is therefore also a responsibility of the consumer to take part in the contract so that he knows what he is signing. Mr President, I would like to There's a lot of us in here who want things to be orderly with regard to phone sales. Not everyone has the courage, so to speak, to throw the phone in the ear of someone who calls and wants to sell a product. It is important that the problem is taken seriously because it is growing. The hope is that a bill on telephone sales can be submitted to Parliament shortly. The Minister for Consumer Affairs has also expressed this in previous debates here in Parliament. The question is currently being prepared at the Department. I and others are looking forward to a proposal that we will have to consider here in Parliament shortly.

i-d299eb6ffd2079c9-988	Mr President, I would like to What is the similarity between a bicycle and a horse? Not much would most people answer, but still the same rules apply if you buy a bicycle in a shop as it is when you buy a horse from a farmer. Of course, there is a big difference between buying a living horse and buying a dead thing, like a car or bicycle. However, EU bureaucrats find it difficult to understand the differences between a large industrial company selling a product produced for the European market and a horse switching owners from one farm to another. The Consumer Sales Law with its rules on flawless goods and the right to compensation is simply not adapted for Swedish conditions where a live animal changes owners. If the seller of the horse happens to be an entrepreneur and the horse is part of the business, which is common since horses are also used as grazing animals in the Swedish landscape, the buyer receives very strong protection while the seller in practice only receives obligations and no rights. For example, the Consumer Sales Law says that an error that appears within six months is assumed to have been there from the beginning unless the company can prove that the error is not original. How do you prove a horse was flawless when it was sold? Even if the animal is vetted, it is of course not a 100% guarantee that there are no so-called errors. Even obvious handling errors will be difficult for a small company to prove that they have occurred. Then, suddenly, the seller may face claims that are much higher than the selling price of the horse. If an animal needs treatment in an animal hospital, this can quickly become large amounts and the previous owner cannot possibly protect himself from these costs. This hampers the market and prevents many small businesses in rural areas from selling to private individuals for fear of what it can lead to. It is also impossible to get out of these rules. Even if one were to try by signing a contract in which one sells the horse as it stands, this agreement does not apply. The Swedish Democrats are aware that the sale of live animals cannot be dealt with as the sale of other goods and therefore it is a problem that needs to be resolved. That is why we support an announcement to the government that the Consumer Sales Law in this area should be more appropriate. Mr President, I would like to Fraud and fraud have become a major social problem affecting both individuals and businesses. Unserious entrepreneurship is a grateful market for criminals, not least because there is hardly any punishment to talk about in this area, at least not in practice when very few people are prosecuted. The Swedish Democrats have proposed a warning list of rogue companies, while the other parties are content with the warning lists available from private actors. However, we see a need for a list that has a clear regulatory framework and that our authorities can have as a basis for refusing, for example, rogue companies to use the Swedish Enforcement Authority for recovery. Today, rogue companies are turning to the Swedish Enforcement Authority to recover debts that are most likely not made in a fair way, but where the person who is to pay does not object to the debt. If there was a serious warning list with clear criteria for what was in force, it could be the basis for the Swedish Enforcement Authority refusing recovery. We do not say which authority should handle the list but it should be more prioritised and more interesting from a general perspective than running a page such as moneyfromsweden.se that the Swedish Consumer Agency operates. If you compare a page that tells you about cheap ways to send money abroad with a page that helps people know which companies are directly rogue, then at least I think that information about rogue companies is more important. Then, of course, rogue companies should not be allowed to continue their activities undisturbed, but when they are discovered and can be identified, they should be prosecuted as soon as possible. However, this may sometimes take time and, in addition, since companies operating in other countries are often involved, the side can act as a help until the companies have had to close down their activities.
